My sleep might be a little off here and there, but nothing I’d want to overstate. I’m not lying awake at 4 a.m. every day or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been feeling more tired or that it’s harder to get through your day, even if you’ve had enough rest?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I get tired like anybody does after work, but I haven’t really noticed some major drop where I can’t get through the day. At least… nothing that stands out enough that I’d want to say yes to that, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty about things lately, or maybe feeling like you’re not worth much or that you’re letting people down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really in that way. I mean, I’ll feel bad if I snap at someone or don’t do something around the house, but I don’t think I’m sitting around thinking I’m worthless or anything like that. I hope that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me a bit more about how you’ve been feeling about the things you used to enjoy — like your morning runs or cooking dinner — have you completely lost interest in them, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it does come up there a bit. I wouldn’t say I’ve completely lost interest, exactly, but… the stuff I used to look forward to doesn’t feel the same.\n\nLike I used to actually want to get up and go for a run in the morning, or mess around on the guitar, or cook dinner after work. Lately I keep skipping those things, and it’s not because I hate them or anything. They just feel like chores, like something I have to force myself to care about. I don’t know if that counts as losing interest, but that’s probably the best way I can explain it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant drop in your energy levels or felt more fatigued lately, even when doing things that normally wouldn’t wear you out?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I wouldn’t say it’s been a significant drop. I mean, I have days where I’m kind of worn out, sure, but nothing where normal stuff suddenly feels exhausting or anything like that. At least not that I’ve really noticed. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're having a harder time making decisions, or that your thoughts feel slowed down? Or maybe you've noticed that your physical movements feel more sluggish or delayed than usual?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but at work a couple people have mentioned I seem kind of slow getting going in briefings… like I’m taking longer to respond than I normally would.\n\nIt’s not like I can’t do my job or anything, but sometimes it feels like my body and my mouth are both a step behind everybody else. I wasn’t sure if that was the kind of thing you meant.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in the things you used to enjoy, like your morning runs or cooking dinner, or does the interest come and go?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s completely gone, no… it’s more like I just don’t reach for those things the way I used to.\n\nI used to look forward to my morning runs, or playing guitar a bit, or making dinner, and lately I keep putting them off. They just feel like chores now, like something I should do but can’t quite get myself to care about. I don’t know if that’s the right way to put it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. My sleep might be a little off here and there, but nothing I’d want to overstate. I’m not lying awake at 4 a.m. every day or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve been feeling more tired or that it’s harder to get through your day, even if you’ve had enough rest?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a big way. I mean, I get tired like anybody does after work, but I haven’t really noticed some major drop where I can’t get through the day. At least… nothing that stands out enough that I’d want to say yes to that, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more guilty about things lately, or maybe feeling like you’re not worth much or that you’re letting people down?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really in that way. I mean, I’ll feel bad if I snap at someone or don’t do something around the house, but I don’t think I’m sitting around thinking I’m worthless or anything like that. I hope that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me a bit more about how you’ve been feeling about the things you used to enjoy — like your morning runs or cooking dinner — have you completely lost interest in them, or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it does come up there a bit. I wouldn’t say I’ve completely lost interest, exactly, but… the stuff I used to look forward to doesn’t feel the same.\n\nLike I used to actually want to get up and go for a run in the morning, or mess around on the guitar, or cook dinner after work. Lately I keep skipping those things, and it’s not because I hate them or anything. They just feel like chores, like something I have to force myself to care about. I don’t know if that counts as losing interest, but that’s probably the best way I can explain it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant drop in your energy levels or felt more fatigued lately, even when doing things that normally wouldn’t wear you out?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I wouldn’t say it’s been a significant drop.
